# C24 programme acceptance ledger

**Status:** The prior C24.01/C24.02 supported closures are determined by their actual completion/admission records; older conditional wording in their unchanged row sections is historical. C24.03 implementer evidence is prepared. Its independent acceptance and closure are established only by the actual `.lit-review/setup/c24-03-completion-20261026.json` and linked originals when present. Candidate-stage static/review outcomes are not predicted.

The [channel signature audit](c24-channel-signatures.json) contains the actual 62 C22/C23 exports. Together with the unchanged [70-name quantitative audit](c24-quantitative-signatures.json) and [82-name products/source audit](c24-products-source-signatures.json), the distinct audited union is 214: all 210 post-release additions and four retained support interfaces. These are audit snapshots, not a new retained baseline. Source links are repository-relative, with named mathlib anchors pinned to the recorded dependency revision.

## Scope and evidence boundary

The complete crosswalk retains **260 execution criteria plus 16 named C9 requirements = 276**, all **216 stable row IDs** and every criterion mapping. All **148 prior row objects and their detailed/process sections are preserved**. This step enriches **43 existing rows: 23 C22, 18 C23 and X-05/X-06**. The count **191** describes implementer-assessed rows, not independent original acceptances. **25** historical/process rows remain `NOT_YET_ASSESSED` for C24.09.

The C24.03 original must independently assess **47 IDs**: all 43 new rows plus current applicability of the unchanged X-01–X-04 under the native INTEGRATION criterion. This does not rewrite those earlier row objects or replace any original report. The [programme comparison with the first release](c24-programme-comparison.md) gives the separate root-authored scope and baseline account; native SCOPE qualification still belongs to the actual original report.

## Current C24.03 evidence and completion boundaries

The parent freshly passed the maintained **17-consumer** focused build in **236.62 seconds**, using existing incremental caches. The maintained compiled import check also passed on its eight selected owners. Exact commands, log digests and unchanged-input identities are in the JSON evidence registry. Read-only comparison matches the actual maintained **124-file plus nine-dependency** trust identity; this reuses the actual C24.01 trust outcome and is not a new C24.03 full trust run. The two corrected fixture files were outside that maintained input map, remain separately evidenced, and receive no exemption from actual code checks.

The reference record distinguishes the parent's selected fresh text/visual readings from registered adaptations and earlier readings. Contracts retain finite/nonempty distinctions, all result letI instances, arbitrary correlated encoder pushforwards, the same code/error/rate family, and eventual versus frequent obstruction boundaries. Source/private names are distinguished from exported constants. Header and hash evidence supplement compiler and same-object proof evidence.

## Entry and immutable baseline

C23 is closed by `cf7340dfd6da41da94058a8375e224d97f93bcb7e4a78e060a7481d480e47cdb` at checkpoint `b577679892f4db40cfad480e0f487184ed91d4d5`. The fresh entry audit matched all 292 original source/environment entries and 33 linked hash/byte checks, and read the original 21-criterion report and all-criterion parent reconciliation. Seven failed observations remain historical failures. The two C24 planning files are intentional additions; complete Git/snapshot equality is not claimed.

The immutable release is `0bef5ef5124d7c33afc1aaed8d4f34a1c3a5ce8f`, annotated tag object `bcd9090ea2720fe14b0a3e168c76ebeef1dafd47`. Its 601 declarations in 31 owners compare with 811 declarations in 57 current owners: 210 additions, no removed names, 92 root exports and 94 reviewed simp names. This inventory is not a semantic-preservation proof or a claim of doubled mathematical scope. The current retained-type checker protects the historical 601; full new-release retention is later C24 work.

## Current checks and historical applicability

| Evidence | C24.01 treatment |
| --- | --- |
| Quantitative consumers | Fresh warning-as-error build of the 14 named permanent consumer modules; existing incremental caches. |
| Current retained/API/import/root/simp/trust boundaries | Fresh maintained trust gate, including its own compatibility rebuild/export and complete current audit. |
| Topology instance integration | Separate fresh FiniteTopology-only and full-Shannon probes: actual inherited topology by `rfl`, compactness from `Finite`, empty/Unit/Bool cases, coordinate continuity and convergence. |
| Exact audited statements | Fresh 70-name structural export using the existing manifest-driven Lean exporter; source hashes and consumer applications remain necessary semantic evidence. |
| C9 growth campaigns and earlier closeouts | Original records checked for provenance and current mechanism relevance; real, synthetic and failed observations remain distinct. No blanket rerun of unchanged campaigns. |
| Current growth-fixture correction | Preserve the original four-error result; corrected current/legacy construction, exact diagnostics, prior approval evidence and refusal/restoration tests pass. Production policy is untouched; full live mutation campaigns are historical. |
| Static/documents and preservation | Current static/generated/site checks and exact crosswalk/link/source/preservation checks are retained in the private C24.01 completion chain. |
| Clean candidate, actual API gate and external package | Later C24 milestones; C23 or historical API evidence does not qualify a changed C24 candidate. |

Exact commands, exits, log digests, cache limits and source hashes are in the companion. Raw originals and validation logs remain private. The current source uses Lean/mathlib v4.33.1 with unchanged pins and only the approved axioms `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`.

## C9–C15 mathematical/interface rows

<a id="c24-e-c09-01"></a>
### C24-E-C09-01 — Arbitrary-type independence under deterministic right processing

**Approved requirements:** `C9-MATH-01`, `C9-CONSUMER-01`, `C9.05-R1`, `C9.05-R2`.

IsIndependentOf p X Y implies IsIndependentOf p X (fun w => f (Y w)); four independent universes, arbitrary source and observed types, no finite/measurable/injective premises.

**Assumptions and edges:** Generic products and actual Nat halving; constant image; left processing through existing symmetry; finite zero-MI consequence, without a new public wrapper.

**Public interface and focused imports:**

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.isIndependentOf_comp_right` — [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/Independence.lean:219](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/Independence.lean#L219); import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SemanticBridge.Independence`.

**Permanent consumers:**

- [LeanInfoTheory/Examples/IndependenceProcessing.lean](../../LeanInfoTheory/Examples/IndependenceProcessing.lean): `product_coordinates`, `product_right`, `nat_halving`, `constant_image`, `left_processing`, `finite_zero_mi`.

**Producer/source connection:** Existing IsIndependentOf mapped-product characterization; PMF.map composition and indepProd.

**Source adaptation:** CT91 §2.8 printed 32–33/PDF54–55: deterministic processing context. PMF mapped-law proof is more general than finite information statements.

**Current disposition:** parent source/consumer assessment and fresh compiled evidence prepared; independent acceptance remains conditional on the actual C24.01 completion chain.

<a id="c24-e-c09-02"></a>
### C24-E-C09-02 — Finite information decomposition through an auxiliary variable

**Approved requirements:** `C9-MATH-02`, `C9-CONSUMER-02`, `C9.06-R1`, `C9.06-R2`.

I(Y;Z)=H(Y|X)-I(X;Z|Y)-H(Y|(X,Z))+I(X;Z), in nats; arbitrary source type, exactly three observed Fintype alphabets, ordered pair (X,Z).

**Assumptions and edges:** Generic zero-term specialization assumes precisely its two zero facts; singleton auxiliary and sparse Boolean triple use same law/variables. No PFR predicate or structural equivalence enters production.

**Public interface and focused imports:**

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.mutualInfoOf_condEntropyOf_decomposition` — [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/InfoMeasures.lean:1128](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/InfoMeasures.lean#L1128); import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.InfoMeasures`.

**Permanent consumers:**

- [LeanInfoTheory/Examples/InformationDecomposition.lean](../../LeanInfoTheory/Examples/InformationDecomposition.lean): `general_decomposition`, `zero_terms`, `singleton_auxiliary`, `sparse_masses`, `sparse_decomposition`.

**Producer/source connection:** Existing entropy/MI/CMI algebra, swap and association identities in lightweight InfoMeasures.

**Source adaptation:** CT91 §§2.4–2.5 printed19–22/PDF41–44; derived algebraic identity, not a separately numbered source theorem.

**Current disposition:** parent source/consumer assessment and fresh compiled evidence prepared; independent acceptance remains conditional on the actual C24.01 completion chain.

## Six producer–consumer connections

| Stable row / responsible step | Contract and present disposition |
| --- | --- |
| `C24-X-01` / `C24.01` | Maximal coupling → two Fano applications → entropy continuity. The same maximalCoupling p q and its coordinate swap have exact marginals and identity-decoder errors TV(p,q). Existing Fano bounds both conditional entropies; the two chain identities and nonnegativity bound both signs of the entropy difference. Equal laws, singleton alphabets and sparse laws retain the same object/error conventions. **SATISFIED** (parent assessment; independent closure conditional). |
| `C24-X-02` / `C24.02` | Dependent probability products → information semantics. Same-coordinate restriction/reindexing and family-law independence; preserve the probability-only import boundary and independent universes. **SATISFIED** (proposed parent source/compiler assessment; independent acceptance only through actual C24.02 completion). |
| `C24-X-03` / `C24.02` | Supported iid products → finite-iid AEP. Supported product-log additivity, actual coordinate laws and varying-space iid independence/integrability; no off-support logarithm substitution. **SATISFIED** (proposed parent source/compiler assessment; independent acceptance only through actual C24.02 completion). |
| `C24-X-04` / `C24.02` | Strict typical sets → one source-code family. The same typical set, natural budget, selected-set code, success/error law and rate produce a witness before tolerance choices; retain rounding, zero-entropy/sparse cases and arbitrary-family converses. **SATISFIED** (proposed parent source/compiler assessment; independent acceptance only through actual C24.02 completion). |
| `C24-X-05` / `C24.03` | Compactness/continuity and arbitrary-input MI → attained capacity. Maximize the actual objective, use actual correlated-input marginals for the upper bound and coordinate maximizers for the lower bound. **SATISFIED** (implementer source/consumer assessment; independent acceptance only through the actual C24.03 completion/original). |
| `C24-X-06` / `C24.03` | Actual channel-code law/error/rate → Fano weak converse. Encoder pushforwards may be nonuniform and correlated; compose actual code law, Markov/DPI, repeated capacity, uniform-message Fano and shared codeRate. Derive eventual boundedness before real limsup; retain eventual/frequent distinction. **SATISFIED** (implementer source/consumer assessment; independent acceptance only through the actual C24.03 completion/original). |

## C16–C21 mathematical/interface rows and selected connections

### C24-E-C16-01 — The constructor, atom and support contracts of Section 3.1 hold with the stated universes and assumptions, including normalized mass, zero atoms and no independent nonemptiness premises.

**Approved criteria:** `C16.01-LAW`

**Contract:** The normalized dependent PMF has ENNReal atom equal to the coordinate product and support exactly coordinatewise support.

**Hypotheses and universes:** I : Type u and A : I -> Type v, with independent u/v; [Fintype I] and [forall i, Fintype (A i)], supplied p : forall i, PMF (A i). No public DecidableEq, Nonempty, measurable-space, positivity or full-support premise. One dependent family shares fiber universe v.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `PMF.finiteProduct` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:45](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L45); kind `def`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
noncomputable def finiteProduct (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) : PMF ((i : I) → A i)
```

- `PMF.finiteProduct_apply` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:54](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L54); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem finiteProduct_apply (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) (x : (i : I) → A i) :
    finiteProduct p x = ∏ i, p i (x i)
```

- `PMF.mem_support_finiteProduct_iff` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:60](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L60); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem mem_support_finiteProduct_iff (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) (x : (i : I) → A i) :
    x ∈ (finiteProduct p).support ↔ ∀ i, x i ∈ (p i).support
```

**Producer/source connection:** finiteProduct uses PMF.ofFintype, Fintype.prod_sum and each component tsum_coe; support uses prod_ne_zero_iff. No duplicate normalization structure.

**Producer anchors:** `finiteProduct` at [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:45](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L45); `finiteProduct_apply` at [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:54](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L54); `mem_support_finiteProduct_iff` at [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:60](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L60)

Focused imports: `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.FiniteProduct`.

### C24-E-C16-02 — Coordinate evaluation, empty products, singleton round-trip consumers and products of pure laws have the exact stated laws.

**Approved criteria:** `C16.01-MARGINALS`

**Contract:** Evaluation recovers the actual component law; empty index is pure empty function; pure components give the pure tuple.

**Hypotheses and universes:** I : Type u and A : I -> Type v, with independent u/v; [Fintype I] and [forall i, Fintype (A i)], supplied p : forall i, PMF (A i). No public DecidableEq, Nonempty, measurable-space, positivity or full-support premise. One dependent family shares fiber universe v.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `PMF.finiteProduct_map_eval` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:86](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L86); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem finiteProduct_map_eval (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) (i : I) :
    (finiteProduct p).map (fun x => x i) = p i
```

- `PMF.finiteProduct_of_isEmpty` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:94](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L94); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem finiteProduct_of_isEmpty [IsEmpty I] (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) :
    finiteProduct p = PMF.pure (fun i => isEmptyElim i)
```

- `PMF.finiteProduct_pure` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:103](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L103); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem finiteProduct_pure (a : (i : I) → A i) :
    finiteProduct (fun i => PMF.pure (a i)) = PMF.pure a
```

**Producer/source connection:** Private piSplitAt law and channelJoint_map_fst establish evaluation; empty/pure proofs use exact masses. Consumers prove singleton evaluation and inverse construction.

### C24-E-C16-04 — Injective restriction and equivalence reindexing have the exact dependent target types/directions; nested restrictions and a heterogeneous round trip are proved without strengthening hypotheses. L1b uses the general public restriction law for its singleton target.

**Approved criteria:** `C16.02-TRANSPORT`

**Contract:** Embedding selection has exact fiber A(e j); equivalence reindexing maps by piCongrLeft.symm, definitionally precomposition.

**Hypotheses and universes:** FP plus J : Type w in an independent index universe and [Fintype J]; restriction takes an embedding J -> I, reindexing an equivalence J = I. The dependent target fiber is exactly A (e j), and the map is precomposition. Injectivity is essential.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `PMF.finiteProduct_map_restrict` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:158](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L158); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem finiteProduct_map_restrict
    {I : Type u} {J : Type w} {A : I → Type v}
    [Fintype I] [Fintype J] [∀ i, Fintype (A i)]
    (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) (e : J ↪ I) :
    (finiteProduct p).map (fun x j => x (e j)) =
      finiteProduct (fun j => p (e j))
```

- `PMF.finiteProduct_map_reindex` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:140](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L140); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem finiteProduct_map_reindex
    {I : Type u} {J : Type w} {A : I → Type v}
    [Fintype I] [Fintype J] [∀ i, Fintype (A i)]
    (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) (e : J ≃ I) :
    (finiteProduct p).map (Equiv.piCongrLeft A e).symm =
      finiteProduct (fun j => p (e j))
```

**Producer/source connection:** Restriction splits at the range, then reindexes via Equiv.ofInjective. Consumers compose maps, compare nested/direct selection and map back through the inverse dependent equivalence.

### C24-E-C16-05 — Sum splitting, inverse concatenation consumer and distinct pair projection have the ordered low-level law equalities of Section 3.3, including empty sides and nonidentical/sparse components.

**Approved criteria:** `C16.02-SPLIT`

**Contract:** Sum splitting produces ordered inl/inr constant-channel joint; distinct coordinate pair produces the corresponding ordered binary law.

**Hypotheses and universes:** Ordered sum splitting applies FP with the sum index and retains each inl/inr dependent fiber; component indices may live in separate universes. Ordered pair projection applies FP and additionally needs i != j. These are separate operations: pair projection has no extra target-index parameter or embedding premise.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `PMF.finiteProduct_map_sum` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:180](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L180); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem finiteProduct_map_sum
    {I : Type u} {J : Type w} {A : I ⊕ J → Type v}
    [Fintype I] [Fintype J] [∀ i, Fintype (A i)]
    (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) :
    (finiteProduct p).map (Equiv.sumPiEquivProdPi A) =
      channelJoint (finiteProduct (fun i => p (Sum.inl i)))
        (fun _ => finiteProduct (fun j => p (Sum.inr j)))
```

- `PMF.finiteProduct_map_pair` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean:204](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/FiniteProduct.lean#L204); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem finiteProduct_map_pair
    {I : Type u} {A : I → Type v}
    [Fintype I] [∀ i, Fintype (A i)]
    (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) {i j : I} (hij : i ≠ j) :
    (finiteProduct p).map (fun x => (x i, x j)) =
      channelJoint (p i) (fun _ => p j)
```

**Producer/source connection:** sumPiEquivProdPi plus prod_sum_type proves the whole-law split; pair uses piSplitAt, then evaluation. Inverse concatenation is derived by map_comp.

### C24-E-C16-06 — L5 proves the concrete repeated-coordinate failure; all coordinate-independence-producing interfaces retain their necessary distinctness/injectivity hypotheses and lightweight imports.

**Approved criteria:** `C16.02-REPETITION`

### C24-E-C16-08 — Both exact Fin splitting/concatenation interfaces operate on nonidentical components and specialize to iid; the `1+2` round trip has the prescribed prefix/suffix orientation.

**Approved criteria:** `C16.03-BLOCKS`

**Contract:** General Fin(m+n) products split with appendEquiv.symm into castAdd prefix then natAdd suffix; inverse append reconstructs Fin.append component laws.

**Hypotheses and universes:** A : Type v, [Fintype A], supplied p : PMF A and every natural n, including zero. General split/append accepts nonidentical p : Fin (m+n) -> PMF A or two families of lengths m/n; no positive-length premise.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `PMF.finiteProduct_map_append_symm` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.Iid`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/Iid.lean:41](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/Iid.lean#L41); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem finiteProduct_map_append_symm
    {A : Type v} [Fintype A] (m n : ℕ) (p : Fin (m + n) → PMF A) :
    (finiteProduct p).map (Fin.appendEquiv m n).symm =
      channelJoint
        (finiteProduct (fun i => p (Fin.castAdd n i)))
        (fun _ => finiteProduct (fun j => p (Fin.natAdd m j)))
```

- `PMF.channelJoint_finiteProduct_map_append` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.Iid`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/Iid.lean:56](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/Iid.lean#L56); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem channelJoint_finiteProduct_map_append
    {A : Type v} [Fintype A] {m n : ℕ}
    (p : Fin m → PMF A) (q : Fin n → PMF A) :
    (channelJoint (finiteProduct p) (fun _ => finiteProduct q)).map
        (Fin.appendEquiv m n) = finiteProduct (Fin.append p q)
```

**Producer/source connection:** Forward law composes finiteProduct_map_reindex via finSumFinEquiv and ordered sum split; inverse maps back and uses map_comp. Actual 1+2 law is nonidentical.

- `PMF.productChannel_apply` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.ProductChannel`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/ProductChannel.lean:62](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/ProductChannel.lean#L62); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem productChannel_apply (W : ∀ i, A i → PMF (B i))
    (x : (i : I) → A i) (y : (i : I) → B i) :

```

```lean
theorem mem_support_productChannel_iff (W : ∀ i, A i → PMF (B i))
    (x : (i : I) → A i) (y : (i : I) → B i) :
    y ∈ (productChannel W x).support ↔ ∀ i, y i ∈ (W i (x i)).support
```

    (x : (i : I) → A i) (i : I) :

```

### C24-E-C17-02 — Injective restriction has the exact dependent input/output types; nested restriction, heterogeneous reindexing and repeated-output failure are exercised through P1/P2.

theorem productChannel_map_restrict {J : Type t} [Fintype J]
    (W : ∀ i, A i → PMF (B i)) (x : (i : I) → A i) (e : J ↪ I) :
    (productChannel W x).map (fun y j => y (e j)) =
      productChannel (fun j => W (e j)) (fun j => x (e j))
```

### C24-E-C17-04 — Independent input products produce the exact product of component output laws, with the stated finite-input/output hypotheses and zero masses allowed.

**Approved criteria:** `C17.02-BIND`

**Contract:** Binding the actual input finiteProduct p through productChannel W equals the product of the actual component outputs p i.bind W i.

**Hypotheses and universes:** PC plus [forall i, Fintype (A i)] and supplied input component laws p. The input law is the actual PMF.finiteProduct p, not an arbitrary q represented as one.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `PMF.finiteProduct_bind_productChannel` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.ProductChannel`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/ProductChannel.lean:91](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/ProductChannel.lean#L91); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem finiteProduct_bind_productChannel [∀ i, Fintype (A i)]
    (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) (W : ∀ i, A i → PMF (B i)) :
    (finiteProduct p).bind (productChannel W) =
      finiteProduct (fun i => (p i).bind (W i))
```

**Producer/source connection:** Proof rewrites finite PMF bind atoms, combines coordinate mass products and uses Fintype.prod_sum. Consumer checks nonidentical inputs and stochastic rows.

### C24-E-C17-05 — Sequential product channels equal the coordinatewise composite in sampling order; arbitrary original inputs and finite intermediate/final alphabets suffice, and stochastic order-sensitive consumers pass.

**Approved criteria:** `C17.02-COMPOSITION`

**Contract:** Sampling W then V equals coordinatewise channelComp(W i)(V i), for arbitrary original input fibers and finite intermediate/final fibers.

**Hypotheses and universes:** PC plus C : I -> Type z and [forall i, Fintype (C i)], with independent z, W : A -> PMF B and V : B -> PMF C. Original A fibers remain arbitrary; intermediate B and final C fibers are finite. Sampling order is W then V.

```lean
theorem channelComp_productChannel {C : I → Type z} [∀ i, Fintype (C i)]
    (W : ∀ i, A i → PMF (B i)) (V : ∀ i, B i → PMF (C i)) :
    channelComp (productChannel W) (productChannel V) =
      productChannel (fun i => channelComp (W i) (V i))
```

### C24-E-C17-07 — The repeated channel is a specialization of the same constructor and generic laws; nonconstant-input permutation covariance and prefix/suffix consistency have the stated orientation without input independence.

**Approved criteria:** `C17.03-MEMORYLESS`

**Contract:** memorylessChannel is repeated identical coordinate channel; permutation gives covariance with the permuted input, not invariance for nonconstant input.

Nonidentical W i : A -> PMF B is allowed by the general block consumers before memoryless specialization. PC plus J : Type t in a fourth independent universe, [Fintype J], and an embedding J -> I for restriction or equivalence J = I for derived reindexing. Selected inputs/channels/outputs have exactly the original dependent fibers at e j. The finiteProduct transport is applied to output fibers B, not input fibers A; no finite-input premise is introduced.

### C24-E-C17-10 — For arbitrary input laws, exact coordinate/block joint and output marginal laws, joint atoms/support and sequential sampling compatibility hold; J2/J3 demonstrate correlated inputs and non-product unconditional outputs. J4 supplies the generic source-to-word law without injectivity and the concrete nonconstant `(a,b) ↦ (a,a)` regression with supported collisions and correlated codewords.

**Approved criteria:** `C17.04-JOINT`

**Contract:** Coordinate/block joint projections use the actual restricted input law; output marginals, atoms/support and sequential sampling agree for arbitrary correlated q.

**Hypotheses and universes:** PC plus an actual q : PMF (forall i, A i). q may be correlated, and A remains arbitrary for probability-level joint/evaluation/restriction laws. Injective restriction additionally takes J : Type t, [Fintype J], e : J embedding I.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `PMF.channelJoint_productChannel_map_eval` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.ProductChannel`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/ProductChannel.lean:130](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/ProductChannel.lean#L130); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem channelJoint_productChannel_map_eval
    (q : PMF ((i : I) → A i)) (W : ∀ i, A i → PMF (B i)) (i : I) :
    (channelJoint q (productChannel W)).map (fun xy => (xy.1 i, xy.2 i)) =
      channelJoint (q.map (fun x => x i)) (W i)
```

- `PMF.channelJoint_productChannel_map_restrict` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.ProductChannel`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/ProductChannel.lean:145](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/ProductChannel.lean#L145); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem channelJoint_productChannel_map_restrict {J : Type t} [Fintype J]
    (q : PMF ((i : I) → A i)) (W : ∀ i, A i → PMF (B i)) (e : J ↪ I) :
    (channelJoint q (productChannel W)).map
        (fun xy => ((fun j => xy.1 (e j)), (fun j => xy.2 (e j)))) =
      channelJoint (q.map (fun x j => x (e j)))
        (productChannel (fun j => W (e j)))
```

- `PMF.productChannel_apply` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Probability.ProductChannel`; [LeanInfoTheory/Probability/ProductChannel.lean:62](../../LeanInfoTheory/Probability/ProductChannel.lean#L62); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

**Producer/source connection:** Production maps through channelJoint bind, uses exact row projection, and retains input map. J4 maps independent sourceBits by noninjective encodeBits(a,b)=(a,a), identifies the diagonal input, then transports the same noisy joint and channelExtension endpoints.

### C24-E-C17-11 — Independent-input joint laws equal the transported product of coordinate joints through the existing dependent equivalence; both directions, sparse laws and iid zero/one specializations are permanently exercised.

**Approved criteria:** `C17.04-FACTORIZATION`

**Contract:** The product of coordinate input-output joints, mapped by arrowProdEquivProdArrow, equals channelJoint of product inputs and productChannel; inverse law holds as a derived consumer.

**Hypotheses and universes:** PC plus [forall i, Fintype (A i)] and supplied input component laws p. The input law is the actual PMF.finiteProduct p, not an arbitrary q represented as one.

```lean
theorem finiteProduct_channelJoint_map [∀ i, Fintype (A i)]
    (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) (W : ∀ i, A i → PMF (B i)) :
    (finiteProduct (fun i => channelJoint (p i) (W i))).map
        (Equiv.arrowProdEquivProdArrow I A B) =
      channelJoint (finiteProduct p) (productChannel W)
```

### C24-E-C17-12 — S1/S2 reach the existing family/independence/binary/conditional APIs. ```lean
theorem channelJoint_eq_iff_eq_on_support
    {alpha : Type u} {beta : Type v}
    (p : PMF alpha) (W V : alpha -> PMF beta) :
    channelJoint p W = channelJoint p V ↔
      ∀ a, a ∈ p.support -> W a = V a
```

```lean
theorem channelJoint_condFstGivenSndChannel
    {alpha : Type u} {beta : Type v} [Fintype alpha]
    (p : PMF (alpha × beta)) :
    PMF.channelJoint (sndMarginal p) (condFstGivenSndChannel p) =
      p.map Prod.swap
```

### C24-E-C18-01 — Product and iid entropy satisfy Section 3.1 with heterogeneous families, separate universes, and existing mutual-independence reuse.

**Approved criteria:** `C18.01-ENTROPY`

**Contract:** Entropy of actual finiteProduct is sum of component entropies; iid is n times base entropy for all n, in nats.

**Hypotheses and universes:** I : Type u and A : I -> Type v, with independent u/v; [Fintype I] and [forall i, Fintype (A i)], supplied p : forall i, PMF (A i). No public DecidableEq, Nonempty, measurable-space, positivity or full-support premise. One dependent family shares fiber universe v. A : Type v, [Fintype A], supplied p : PMF A and every natural n, including zero. General split/append accepts nonidentical p : Fin (m+n) -> PMF A or two families of lengths m/n; no positive-length premise.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.entropy_finiteProduct` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SemanticBridge.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/FiniteProduct.lean:77](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/FiniteProduct.lean#L77); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem entropy_finiteProduct
    {I : Type u} {A : I → Type v}
    [Fintype I] [∀ i, Fintype (A i)]
    (p : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) :
    entropy (PMF.finiteProduct p) = ∑ i, entropy (p i)
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.entropy_iid` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SemanticBridge.FiniteProduct`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/FiniteProduct.lean:102](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/FiniteProduct.lean#L102); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem entropy_iid
    {A : Type v} [Fintype A] (p : PMF A) (n : ℕ) :
    entropy (PMF.iid p n) = (n : ℝ) * entropy p
```

**Producer/source connection:** The proof identifies full family entropy with word entropy by injective restriction, applies existing equality iff full mutual independence to the C16 bridge, and rewrites exact coordinate marginals.

### C24-E-C18-04 — The binary/splitting checkpoint is resolved, and finite-product and iid KL additivity hold in ENNReal without support restrictions, including top and zero length.

**Approved criteria:** `C18.02-ENNREAL`

**Contract:** Canonical ENNReal product KL equals the sum of coordinate KL, with no support restriction; iid is n times base KL, including top and0*top=0.

**Hypotheses and universes:** FP with numerator/reference component PMFs p/q, plus explicit [forall i, MeasurableSpace (A i)] and [forall i, MeasurableSingletonClass (A i)]; standard Pi measurable structure. Canonical ENNReal result has no support guard. Real form adds forall i, (p i).support subset (q i).support. Iid analog has finite/measurable-singleton base alphabet and adds base support inclusion only for the Real endpoint.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.klDiv_finiteProduct` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SemanticBridge.ProductKL`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductKL.lean:79](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductKL.lean#L79); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem klDiv_finiteProduct
    {I : Type u} {A : I → Type v}
    [Fintype I] [∀ i, Fintype (A i)]
    [∀ i, MeasurableSpace (A i)] [∀ i, MeasurableSingletonClass (A i)]
    (p q : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) :
    InformationTheory.klDiv (PMF.finiteProduct p).toMeasure
        (PMF.finiteProduct q).toMeasure =
      ∑ i, InformationTheory.klDiv (p i).toMeasure (q i).toMeasure
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.klDiv_iid` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SemanticBridge.ProductKL`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductKL.lean:145](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductKL.lean#L145); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem klDiv_iid
    {α : Type v} [Fintype α]
    [MeasurableSpace α] [MeasurableSingletonClass α]
    (p q : PMF α) (n : ℕ) :
    InformationTheory.klDiv (PMF.iid p n).toMeasure (PMF.iid q n).toMeasure =
      (n : ENNReal) * InformationTheory.klDiv p.toMeasure q.toMeasure
```

### C24-E-C18-05 — Real product and iid forms require explicit support/finiteness guards and justify every ENNReal.toReal conversion.

**Approved criteria:** `C18.02-REAL`

**Contract:** Real product sum requires coordinate support inclusion, and iid Real theorem retains base support inclusion as a finite-information contract.

**Hypotheses and universes:** FP with numerator/reference component PMFs p/q, plus explicit [forall i, MeasurableSpace (A i)] and [forall i, MeasurableSingletonClass (A i)]; standard Pi measurable structure. Canonical ENNReal result has no support guard. Real form adds forall i, (p i).support subset (q i).support. Iid analog has finite/measurable-singleton base alphabet and adds base support inclusion only for the Real endpoint.

```lean
theorem toReal_klDiv_finiteProduct
    {I : Type u} {A : I → Type v}
    [Fintype I] [∀ i, Fintype (A i)]
    [∀ i, MeasurableSpace (A i)] [∀ i, MeasurableSingletonClass (A i)]
    (p q : ∀ i, PMF (A i)) (h : ∀ i, (p i).support ⊆ (q i).support) :
    (InformationTheory.klDiv (PMF.finiteProduct p).toMeasure
        (PMF.finiteProduct q).toMeasure).toReal =
      ∑ i, (InformationTheory.klDiv (p i).toMeasure (q i).toMeasure).toReal
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.toReal_klDiv_iid` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SemanticBridge.ProductKL`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductKL.lean:158](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductKL.lean#L158); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem toReal_klDiv_iid
    {α : Type v} [Fintype α]
    [MeasurableSpace α] [MeasurableSingletonClass α]
    (p q : PMF α) (h : p.support ⊆ q.support) (n : ℕ) :
    (InformationTheory.klDiv (PMF.iid p n).toMeasure
        (PMF.iid q n).toMeasure).toReal =
      (n : ℝ) * (InformationTheory.klDiv p.toMeasure q.toMeasure).toReal
```

### C24-E-C18-08 — Output-given-input entropy adds for arbitrary correlated input laws with exactly Section 3.3's assumptions and orientation.

**Approved criteria:** `C18.03-CONDITIONAL`

**Contract:** For any correlated q, H(output|input) is the sum of H(output_i|input_i) under actual q_i and W_i; swap expresses first-given-second convention.

**Hypotheses and universes:** I : Type u, A : I -> Type v, B : I -> Type w; [Fintype I], finite A and B fibers, independently universed. Shannon quantities are Real nats. Arbitrary supplied q and W do not require input independence/full support; equality from product input is separately stated.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.condEntropy_channelJoint_productChannel` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SemanticBridge.ProductChannel`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductChannel.lean:67](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductChannel.lean#L67); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem condEntropy_channelJoint_productChannel
    {I : Type u} {A : I → Type v} {B : I → Type w}
    [Fintype I] [∀ i, Fintype (A i)] [∀ i, Fintype (B i)]
    (q : PMF ((i : I) → A i)) (W : ∀ i, A i → PMF (B i)) :
    condEntropy ((PMF.channelJoint q (PMF.productChannel W)).map Prod.swap) =
      ∑ i, condEntropy
        ((PMF.channelJoint (q.map (fun x => x i)) (W i)).map Prod.swap)
```

**Producer/source connection:** Private row-entropy averaging follows existing MI chain identity; product row entropy adds; finite expectation transport through coordinate map proves the exact actual-marginal sum.

**Producer anchors:** `condEntropy_channelJoint_productChannel` at [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductChannel.lean:67](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductChannel.lean#L67)

**Approved criteria:** `C18.04-BOUND`

**Contract:** Block MI is at most the sum of actual-coordinate MI for every finite input-word law q, including correlated noninjectively encoded sources.

**Hypotheses and universes:** I : Type u, A : I -> Type v, B : I -> Type w; [Fintype I], finite A and B fibers, independently universed. Shannon quantities are Real nats. Arbitrary supplied q and W do not require input independence/full support; equality from product input is separately stated.

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.mutualInfo_channelJoint_productChannel_le` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SemanticBridge.ProductChannel`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductChannel.lean:125](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/ProductChannel.lean#L125); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem mutualInfo_channelJoint_productChannel_le
    {I : Type u} {A : I → Type v} {B : I → Type w}
    [Fintype I] [∀ i, Fintype (A i)] [∀ i, Fintype (B i)]
    (q : PMF ((i : I) → A i)) (W : ∀ i, A i → PMF (B i)) :
    mutualInfo (PMF.channelJoint q (PMF.productChannel W)) ≤
      ∑ i, mutualInfo (PMF.channelJoint (q.map (fun x => x i)) (W i))
```

I : Type u, A : I -> Type v, B : I -> Type w; [Fintype I], finite A and B fibers, independently universed. Shannon quantities are Real nats. Arbitrary supplied q and W do not require input independence/full support; equality from product input is separately stated. A : Type v, [Fintype A], supplied p : PMF A and every natural n, including zero. ```lean
theorem mutualInfo_map_piProd_eq_sum_of_isMutuallyIndependentFamily
    {I : Type u} {A : I → Type v} {B : I → Type w}
    [Fintype I] [∀ i, Fintype (A i)] [∀ i, Fintype (B i)]
    (r : PMF ((i : I) → A i × B i))
    (hr : IsMutuallyIndependentFamily r Finset.univ) :

      ∑ i, mutualInfo (r.map (fun z => z i))
```

```lean
theorem sum_mutualInfo_sub_mutualInfo_productChannel
    {I : Type u} {A : I → Type v} {B : I → Type w}
    [Fintype I] [∀ i, Fintype (A i)] [∀ i, Fintype (B i)]
    (q : PMF ((i : I) → A i)) (W : ∀ i, A i → PMF (B i)) :
    (∑ i, mutualInfo (PMF.channelJoint (q.map (fun x => x i)) (W i))) -

### C24-E-C19-01 — Supported block additivity holds for every length without full support; the permanent sparse impossible-word regression proves that unrestricted additivity fails.

theorem selfInfo_iid_of_mem_support {A : Type u} [Fintype A]
    (p : PMF A) (n : ℕ) {x : Fin n → A} (hx : x ∈ (PMF.iid p n).support) :

**Contract:** The private connected moment checkpoint for Z_n=(sum_i selfInfo p(x_i))/n has MemLp 2, mean H, variance V_p/n, actual block-deviation event equality and both ENNReal finite-event guards.

**Hypotheses and universes:** Type u alphabet A; [Fintype A]; supplied p : PMF A. Public statements add no DecidableEq, measurable-space, Nonempty, full-support or positive-entropy hypothesis. H=entropy p and self-information/rate are Real nats. For normalized mean/variance n>0; epsilon arbitrary here. Internal discrete measurable-space instances are chosen locally. V_p=sum_a p(a).toReal*(selfInfo p a-H)^2.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.weakTypicalSet` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.TypicalSet`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/TypicalSet.lean:40](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/TypicalSet.lean#L40); kind `def`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
def weakTypicalSet {A : Type u} [Fintype A] (p : PMF A) (n : ℕ) (ε : ℝ) :
    Finset (Fin n → A)
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.mem_weakTypicalSet` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.TypicalSet`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/TypicalSet.lean:49](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/TypicalSet.lean#L49); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem mem_weakTypicalSet {A : Type u} [Fintype A] (p : PMF A)
    {n : ℕ} {ε : ℝ} {x : Fin n → A} :
    x ∈ weakTypicalSet p n ε ↔
      0 < n ∧ x ∈ (PMF.iid p n).support ∧
        |selfInfo (PMF.iid p n) x / (n : ℝ) - entropy p| < ε
```

**Producer/source connection:** The definition filters Finset.univ by all three conjunctions. Membership rewrites exactly those conditions; finite atom/cardinality proofs and every later coding construction use this same definition.

    {n : ℕ} {ε : ℝ} {x : Fin n → A} (hx : x ∈ weakTypicalSet p n ε) :

The construction uses [Finite X], with explicit [Nonempty X] only when law-free.     (x : X) : x ∈ c.successSet ↔ c.decoder (c.encoder x) = x
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SourceCode.successProbability_eq_one_sub_errorProbability` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SourceCoding`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SourceCoding.lean:96](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SourceCoding.lean#L96); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem successProbability_eq_one_sub_errorProbability
    {X : Type u} [Fintype X] {M : ℕ} (c : SourceCode X M) (q : PMF X) :
    (q.toOuterMeasure (c.successSet : Set X)).toReal = 1 - c.errorProbability q
```

```lean
theorem decodingErrorProbabilityOf_eq_toOuterMeasure
    {omega : Type u} {alpha : Type v} {beta : Type w}
    (p : PMF omega) (X : omega → alpha) (Y : omega → beta)
    (decoder : beta → alpha) :
    decodingErrorProbabilityOf p X Y decoder =
      (p.toOuterMeasure {ω | decoder (Y ω) ≠ X ω}).toReal
```

**Producer/source connection:** Fano pulls two PMF maps back using map_comp and toOuterMeasure_map_apply. SourceCoding identifies failure as the complement of actual success and uses finite PMF probability complementarity. It does not discard impossible values or extra correct decodings.

**Hypotheses and universes:** X : Type u, M : Nat. Raw pairs and error need no finite/nonempty instance; success enumeration uses [Fintype X]. The construction uses [Finite X], with explicit [Nonempty X] only when law-free. [Fintype X], arbitrary q : PMF X and c : SourceCode X M. ```lean

    (c : SourceCode X M) (q : PMF X) (T : Finset X) (b : ℝ)

      (q.toOuterMeasure (T : Set X)ᶜ).toReal + (M : ℝ) * b
```

### C24-E-C20-11 — Prove the ceiling-bit-length iff and Units conversion. A permanent consumer proves binary cardinality and uses an actual embedding and total decoder extension to preserve a code's recovered output and error, distinguishing `M≤2^k` from `M=2^k`. For minimal `k` it also proves the original-to-padded rate bounds in 3.5 under `1≤M` and `0<n`, privately.

**Approved criteria:** `C20.03-BITS`

**Contract:** For M>=1, ceil(natsToBits(log M))<=k iff M<=2^k, and natsToBits(codeRate n (2^k))=k/n. Actual binary padding preserves recovered outputs and exact error; minimal padding has rate in [original, original+log 2/n).

**Hypotheses and universes:** Public statements use natural M,n,k and explicit M>=1 for the ceiling equivalence; unit equality includes n=0 algebraically. Private padding assumes M>=1 and M<=2^k; strict minimal-rate bound needs n>0.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.ceil_natsToBits_log_le_iff` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.CodingRate`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean:100](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean#L100); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem ceil_natsToBits_log_le_iff {M : ℕ} (hM : 1 ≤ M) (k : ℕ) :
    ⌈natsToBits (Real.log (M : ℝ))⌉₊ ≤ k ↔ M ≤ 2 ^ k
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.natsToBits_codeRate_pow_two` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.CodingRate`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean:110](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean#L110); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem natsToBits_codeRate_pow_two (n k : ℕ) :
    natsToBits (codeRate n (2 ^ k)) = (k : ℝ) / n
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.codeRate` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.CodingRate`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean:32](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean#L32); kind `def`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

**Producer/source connection:** Private consumer builds an equivalence between k-bit words and Fin(2^k), embeds Fin M, and extends a decoder with c.decoder 0 on unused strings. binary_recovered/padded_recovered prove equality of outputs at every source point; generic Fano event semantics gives exact error. Minimal Nat ceiling bounds give strict overhead.

**Producer anchors:** `ceil_natsToBits_log_le_iff` at [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean:100](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean#L100); `natsToBits_codeRate_pow_two` at [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean:110](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean#L110); `decodingErrorProbabilityOf_eq_toOuterMeasure` at [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/Fano.lean:103](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/Fano.lean#L103)

### C24-E-C21-01 — Prove section 3.1 for arbitrary finite p and H<R; one dependent family has all positive budgets, typical-word recovery and complement error bounds in the connected private production checkpoint. The public theorem uses that same witness for error tending to zero and the required eventual rate upper bound; external consumers check these exported guarantees, not hidden properties of an extracted witness.

**Approved criteria:** `C21.01-WITNESS`

**Contract:** For H<R, there exist one positive natural budget family M and one dependent c with error->0 and eventually codeRate<=R. The connected private construction additionally retains the same rounded budget/recovery/complement/rate properties at every length.

**Hypotheses and universes:** Type u alphabet A; [Fintype A]; supplied p : PMF A. Public statements add no DecidableEq, measurable-space, Nonempty, full-support or positive-entropy hypothesis. H=entropy p and self-information/rate are Real nats. M : Nat -> Nat, hM : forall n, 1 <= M n, c : forall n, BlockSourceCode A n (M n). Each n uses its own word type Fin n -> A, actual q_n=PMF.iid p n, e_n=(c n).errorProbability q_n, r_n=codeRate n (M n). Scalar Tendsto is atTop Nat. Existential M,c precede the two public conclusions; H<R is strict.

**Public interfaces and exact written source headers:**

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.exists_blockSourceCode_tendsto_errorProbability_zero` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SourceCodingAsymptotic`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SourceCodingAsymptotic.lean:150](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SourceCodingAsymptotic.lean#L150); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem exists_blockSourceCode_tendsto_errorProbability_zero
    {A : Type u} [Fintype A] (p : PMF A) {R : ℝ} (hR : entropy p < R) :
    ∃ M : ℕ → ℕ, (∀ n, 1 ≤ M n) ∧
      ∃ c : ∀ n, BlockSourceCode A n (M n),
        Tendsto (fun n => (c n).errorProbability (PMF.iid p n)) atTop (nhds 0) ∧
        (∀ᶠ n in atTop, codeRate n (M n) ≤ R)
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.exists_sourceCode_errorProbability_le` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SourceCoding`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SourceCoding.lean:178](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SourceCoding.lean#L178); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.roundedMessageSize_bounds` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.CodingRate`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean:69](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/CodingRate.lean#L69); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.tendsto_prob_weakTypicalSet` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SemanticBridge.AEP`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/AEP.lean:262](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SemanticBridge/AEP.lean#L262); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

**Producer/source connection:** rounded_sourceCode_family fixes epsilon=rho-H and M_n=ceil exp(n*rho), calls the public selected-set constructor on the same T_n at every n, chooses c once, and squeezes its actual error by the actual complement. Public achievability sets rho=(H+R)/2 and exports that same witness error/cap only.

```lean
theorem tendsto_errorProbability_iid_one_of_eventually_codeRate_le
    {A : Type u} [Fintype A] (p : PMF A)
    (M : ℕ → ℕ) (hM : ∀ n, 1 ≤ M n) (c : ∀ n, BlockSourceCode A n (M n))
    {R : ℝ} (hR : R < entropy p)
    (hrate : ∀ᶠ n in atTop, codeRate n (M n) ≤ R) :
    Tendsto (fun n => (c n).errorProbability (PMF.iid p n)) atTop (nhds 1)
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.eventually_entropy_sub_le_codeRate_of_tendsto_errorProbability_zero` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SourceCodingAsymptotic`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SourceCodingAsymptotic.lean:245](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SourceCodingAsymptotic.lean#L245); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem eventually_entropy_sub_le_codeRate_of_tendsto_errorProbability_zero
    {A : Type u} [Fintype A] (p : PMF A)
    (M : ℕ → ℕ) (hM : ∀ n, 1 ≤ M n) (c : ∀ n, BlockSourceCode A n (M n))
    (herror : Tendsto (fun n => (c n).errorProbability (PMF.iid p n)) atTop (nhds 0))
    {δ : ℝ} (hδ : 0 < δ) :
    ∀ᶠ n in atTop, entropy p - δ ≤ codeRate n (M n)
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.sourceCoding_threshold` — owner/minimal import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.SourceCodingAsymptotic`; [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SourceCodingAsymptotic.lean:276](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/SourceCodingAsymptotic.lean#L276); kind `theorem`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem sourceCoding_threshold {A : Type u} [Fintype A] (p : PMF A) :
    IsGLB {R : ℝ | ∃ M : ℕ → ℕ, (∀ n, 1 ≤ M n) ∧
      ∃ c : ∀ n, BlockSourceCode A n (M n),
        Tendsto (fun n => (c n).errorProbability (PMF.iid p n)) atTop (nhds 0) ∧
        (∀ η : ℝ, 0 < η → ∀ᶠ n in atTop, codeRate n (M n) ≤ R + η)}
      (entropy p)
```

H=entropy p and self-information/rate are Real nats. M : Nat -> Nat, hM : forall n, 1 <= M n, c : forall n, BlockSourceCode A n (M n). Each n uses its own word type Fin n -> A, actual q_n=PMF.iid p n, e_n=(c n).errorProbability q_n, r_n=codeRate n (M n). Scalar Tendsto is atTop Nat. Above-H witness uses epsilon=rho-H>0 and rho between H and target R. Arbitrary-family converses have their separate exact hypotheses; threshold puts witnesses before all upper tolerances.

Lean instead uses nats, arbitrary positive natural message budgets M and total deterministic decoders with no erasure output or reserved message. Replace erasure by an ambient source value; all M natural indices available. Extra successes outside T allowed, hence error<=complement, not equality or optimal erasure-tail identity. The reference-register C21.01 phrase that the source uses nats describes library source, not every PW24 passage.
- `PW24-rounding`: 11.1-11.2 source-coding rate discussion. Natural M=ceil exp(nR), nats log(M)/n, explicit positive-length guards, nonnegative-target rate sandwich and exact binary-padding/error preservation. No informal nonintegral message budget or erasure index.

Arbitrary-T success splitting and atom-cap bound generalized for arbitrary finite law, total decoder and natural budget. Source strong converse uses actual strict typicality and positive-gap decay. No channel strong converse or error exponent.
- `C21-composition`: Approved C21 revision 2 sections 3.1-3.5; public owner and permanent consumer. One dependent family before upper tolerances; source strong converse differs from arbitrary-family eventual necessary-rate converse. IsGLB is derived from above-H achievability plus general converse, not a newly attributed numbered textbook theorem. H membership/exact-rate-H, error exponents and information-spectrum frameworks are not claimed.

<a id="c24-e-c22-01"></a>
### C24-E-C22-01 — Define precisely section 3.1's supremum and prove the objective bound and universal-upper-bound iff, with genuine nonempty and bounded range evidence for Real supremum operations. Perform section 4's early signature check, including the explicit retained `[Nonempty A]` binder.

**Approved requirements:** `C22.01-CAPACITY`.

Capacity is precisely the real supremum of the actual channel-joint MI range, with nonempty and bounded range evidence before each conditional-supremum operation.

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u and B : Type v, independent universes; [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B], supplied W : A -> PMF B. channelCapacity explicitly binds Nonempty A and equals the Real sSup of the range of p |-> mutualInfo(channelJoint p W) over ALL PMF A. No Nonempty B, DecidableEq, support/positivity, optimizer, compactness or continuity premise is added. Nats. Canonical Real information/capacity in nats; bits only through the explicit existing Units conversion.

**Edges and distinctions:** Explicit Nonempty A is in the definition header; no empty-supremum default or chosen-optimizer object. Exact elaborated retained binder remains a parent type-export obligation.

**Public declarations and focused owners:**

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.channelCapacity` — [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/ChannelCapacity.lean:38](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/ChannelCapacity.lean#L38); import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.ChannelCapacity`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
noncomputable def channelCapacity {A : Type u} {B : Type v}
    [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B] (W : A → PMF B) : ℝ
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.mutualInfo_channelJoint_le_channelCapacity` — [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/ChannelCapacity.lean:56](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/ChannelCapacity.lean#L56); import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.ChannelCapacity`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem mutualInfo_channelJoint_le_channelCapacity (p : PMF A) (W : A → PMF B) :
    mutualInfo (PMF.channelJoint p W) ≤ channelCapacity W
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.channelCapacity_le_iff` — [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/ChannelCapacity.lean:61](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/ChannelCapacity.lean#L61); import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.ChannelCapacity`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem channelCapacity_le_iff (W : A → PMF B) (r : ℝ) :
    channelCapacity W ≤ r ↔ ∀ p : PMF A, mutualInfo (PMF.channelJoint p W) ≤ r
```

    (p : PMF Bool) (ε : NNReal) (hε : ε ≤ 1) :

```

<a id="c24-e-c23-01"></a>
### C24-E-C23-01 — Deliver the transparent block pair with the exact encoder/decoder orientations, explicit positive-budget operational interface, independent alphabet universes and no recovery, injectivity or independence fields.

**Approved requirements:** `C23.01-MODEL`.

BlockChannelCode A B n M is exactly (Fin M -> (Fin n -> A)) x ((Fin n -> B) -> Fin M); encoder/decoder are the two projections. Operational positivity is explicit and no validity fields are built into the pair.

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u and B : Type v in independent universes; n,M : Nat. BlockChannelCode and encoder/decoder need no typeclasses. Operational law/error statements require only [Fintype B], W : A -> PMF B, actual c and hM : 1<=M. They derive Nonempty(Fin M) locally and impose no finite/nonempty A, injectivity, recovery or input-independence hypothesis. Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

**Edges and distinctions:** A raw pair carries no promise of recovery. Raw n=0 laws can use empty base types; this does not define their base channel capacity. No Nonempty A or Fintype A is added to the raw interface.

<a id="c24-e-c23-02"></a>
### C24-E-C23-02 — Construct `J` and `Q` and prove all message/input/output and pair-marginal identifications in Section 3.1 using existing raw PMF channel constructions, retaining message identity under collisions.

**Approved requirements:** `C23.01-LAWS`.

For U uniform on Fin M, K=memorylessChannel W n and q=U.map c.encoder, J=channelJoint U (K o encoder) and Q=channelExtension (channelJoint U (deterministicChannel encoder)) K. All seven message/input/output/pair marginals identify those SAME laws.

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u and B : Type v in independent universes; n,M : Nat. BlockChannelCode and encoder/decoder need no typeclasses. Operational law/error statements require only [Fintype B], W : A -> PMF B, actual c and hM : 1<=M. They derive Nonempty(Fin M) locally and impose no finite/nonempty A, injectivity, recovery or input-independence hypothesis. Q has right-associated type Fin M x ((Fin n -> A) x (Fin n -> B)); J : PMF(Fin M x (Fin n -> B)). Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

**Edges and distinctions:** The encoder image law is not assumed uniform: unequal fibers give masses 2/3 and 1/3. The word input law is not iid: the repeated-word fixture has fair marginals but zero mixed-word mass. No conditioning by a zero-mass fiber or quotienting message indices occurs.

```

<a id="c24-e-c23-03"></a>
### C24-E-C23-03 — Identify the same error with the existing Fano PMF/RV/event definitions and the uniform finite average formula; prove its range without a replacement indicator or unjustified probability division.

**Approved requirements:** `C23.01-ERROR`.

Pe is exactly decodingErrorProbability J decoder, equals decodingErrorProbabilityOf Q message output decoder and the real mass of {z | decoder(z.output) != z.message}. It is (1/M) times the finite sum of conditional output error masses, and lies in [0,1].

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u and B : Type v in independent universes; n,M : Nat. BlockChannelCode and encoder/decoder need no typeclasses. Operational law/error statements require only [Fintype B], W : A -> PMF B, actual c and hM : 1<=M. They derive Nonempty(Fin M) locally and impose no finite/nonempty A, injectivity, recovery or input-independence hypothesis. Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

**Edges and distinctions:** Uniform averaging is over original messages, not distinct codewords. hM>0 protects the operational uniform law and denominator. Pe=1 is a valid result, not an excluded degeneracy.

**Public declarations and focused owners:**

<a id="c24-e-c23-04"></a>
### C24-E-C23-04 — Prove and independently consume `M=1` zero error, arbitrary-positive-budget `n=0` error `1-1/M`, and a one-letter law/error reduction. **Approved requirements:** `C23.01-BOUNDARIES`.

Every M=1 code has Pe=0. At n=0 with positive M, Pe=1-1/M. At n=1 the output evaluation gives the original channel joint law and the finite error sum over B. Unequal Fin3 fibers retain input masses 2/3,1/3 and actual error 1/3.

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u and B : Type v in independent universes; n,M : Nat. BlockChannelCode and encoder/decoder need no typeclasses. Operational law/error statements require only [Fintype B], W : A -> PMF B, actual c and hM : 1<=M. They derive Nonempty(Fin M) locally and impose no finite/nonempty A, injectivity, recovery or input-independence hypothesis. Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

**Edges and distinctions:** Channel-code n=0 error is 1-1/M, unlike a source-code empty-word error of zero. Empty Empty zero-word M=3 has error 2/3 without evaluating capacity. Unused decoder indices can correspond to positive-mass messages.

<a id="c24-e-c23-05"></a>
### C24-E-C23-05 — Establish the two actual-law Markov chains and both information-processing inequalities of Section 3.2, with correct coordinate order and no null-fiber interpretation.

**Approved requirements:** `C23.02-PROCESSING`.

On the actual Q: message -> encoded input -> output is Markov; message -> output -> decoded message is Markov. Thus I(message;decoded)<=I(message;output)<=I(input;output).

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u and B : Type v in independent universes; n,M : Nat. BlockChannelCode and encoder/decoder need no typeclasses. Operational law/error statements require only [Fintype B], W : A -> PMF B, actual c and hM : 1<=M. They derive Nonempty(Fin M) locally and impose no finite/nonempty A, injectivity, recovery or input-independence hypothesis. The Markov facts and decoder DPI need only [Fintype B]. Message-to-input DPI additionally needs [Fintype A], but no Nonempty A or capacity hypothesis. Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

**Edges and distinctions:** No conditional PMF is assigned significance at a null fiber. DPI orientation is output->input->message after reversal, then MI symmetry restores the displayed order. No unrelated abstract Markov witness is substituted for Q.

The pointwise theorem does NOT require C<R. Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

<a id="c24-e-c23-09"></a>
### C24-E-C23-09 — Derive eventual rate boundedness from the finite bound and vanishing error, with an explicit finite bound and lower/coboundedness justification; no bounded-rate or rate-convergence premise may appear in the result or be hidden in a helper's use.

**Approved requirements:** `C23.03-BOUNDEDNESS`.

Vanishing actual error derives eventually r_n <= 2*(C+log2); nonnegative rates provide the lower control needed by real limsup.

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u, B : Type v; [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B], W : A -> PMF B, actual c : BlockChannelCode A B n M and hM : 1<=M. Output nonemptiness is not an additional public premise. C=channelCapacity W, Pe=c.errorProbability W hM, r=codeRate n M, all information/rates in Real nats. Family data: M : Nat -> Nat, hM : forall n, 1<=M n, c : forall n, BlockChannelCode A B n (M n); one fixed W. e_n=(c n).errorProbability W (hM n), r_n=codeRate n (M n). Limits, eventually and frequently are along Filter.atTop on ALL natural lengths. Assumption only Tendsto e atTop (nhds 0), apart from positive budgets and finite-channel hypotheses. Derived boundedness remains a private helper, not a public premise. Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

**Edges and distinctions:** No helper assumes rate boundedness, convergence or a limit for e_n*r_n. The bound is eventual, so no restriction on a finite initial segment is imposed. Rate nonnegativity holds also at n=0 with positive budgets.

**Public declarations and focused owners:**

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.BlockChannelCode.eventually_codeRate_le_channelCapacity_add` — [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/ChannelCodingAsymptotic.lean:75](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/ChannelCodingAsymptotic.lean#L75); import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.ChannelCodingAsymptotic`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem eventually_codeRate_le_channelCapacity_add
    [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B]
    (W : A → PMF B) (M : ℕ → ℕ) (hM : ∀ n, 1 ≤ M n)
    (c : ∀ n, BlockChannelCode A B n (M n))
    (he : Tendsto (fun n => (c n).errorProbability W (hM n)) atTop (nhds 0))
    (δ : ℝ) (hδ : 0 < δ) :
    ∀ᶠ n in atTop, codeRate n (M n) ≤ channelCapacity W + δ
```

- `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.BlockChannelCode.limsup_codeRate_le_channelCapacity` — [LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/ChannelCodingAsymptotic.lean:103](../../LeanInfoTheory/Shannon/ChannelCodingAsymptotic.lean#L103); import `LeanInfoTheory.Shannon.ChannelCodingAsymptotic`; reviewed attributes `[]`.

```lean
theorem limsup_codeRate_le_channelCapacity
    [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B]
    (W : A → PMF B) (M : ℕ → ℕ) (hM : ∀ n, 1 ≤ M n)
    (c : ∀ n, BlockChannelCode A B n (M n))
    (he : Tendsto (fun n => (c n).errorProbability W (hM n)) atTop (nhds 0)) :

### C24-E-C23-13 — Under eventual `R ≤ r n` and `C(W)<R`, prove the exact eventual quantitative lower bound in Section 3.4, an explicit eventual positive error floor and failure of vanishing error, deriving `R>0` rather than assuming a bounded rate family.

**Approved requirements:** `C23.04-EVENTUAL`.

If C<R and eventually R<=r_n, then eventually 1-C/R-log2/(nR)<=e_n; the explicit floor (1-C/R)/2 is positive and eventually <=e_n; hence error cannot tend to zero.

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u, B : Type v; [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B], W : A -> PMF B, actual c : BlockChannelCode A B n M and hM : 1<=M. Output nonemptiness is not an additional public premise. C=channelCapacity W, Pe=c.errorProbability W hM, r=codeRate n M, all information/rates in Real nats. Family data: M : Nat -> Nat, hM : forall n, 1<=M n, c : forall n, BlockChannelCode A B n (M n); one fixed W. e_n=(c n).errorProbability W (hM n), r_n=codeRate n (M n). Limits, eventually and frequently are along Filter.atTop on ALL natural lengths. Arbitrary real R with C<R and eventual lower-rate premise. No vanishing-error assumption, bounded/convergent-rate premise or explicit R>0 assumption. Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

**Edges and distinctions:** Only a weak-converse floor/nonvanishing conclusion; no error->1 channel strong converse. Positive blocklength is only a tail condition; arbitrary finite initial values stay allowed.

```lean
theorem eventually_one_sub_channelCapacity_div_sub_le_errorProbability
    [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B]
    (W : A → PMF B) (M : ℕ → ℕ) (hM : ∀ n, 1 ≤ M n)
    (c : ∀ n, BlockChannelCode A B n (M n))
    {R : ℝ} (hR : channelCapacity W < R)
    (hrate : ∀ᶠ n in atTop, R ≤ codeRate n (M n)) :
    ∀ᶠ n : ℕ in atTop, 1 - channelCapacity W / R - Real.log 2 / ((n : ℝ) * R) ≤
      (c n).errorProbability W (hM n)
```

### C24-E-C23-14 — Under a fixed positive rate gap infinitely often, prove failure of vanishing error for arbitrary possibly unbounded rates, using the explicit frequent formulation and no eventual-above-capacity premise or unguarded real limsup.

**Approved requirements:** `C23.04-FREQUENT`.

For eta>0, frequent C+eta<=r_n implies error does not tend to zero, even when the rates are unbounded or nonconvergent.

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u, B : Type v; [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B], W : A -> PMF B, actual c : BlockChannelCode A B n M and hM : 1<=M. Limits, eventually and frequently are along Filter.atTop on ALL natural lengths. Fixed eta>0 and explicit Filter.Frequently atTop premise; no eventual-above-capacity assumption, real-limsup comparison or rate bound. Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

<a id="c24-e-c23-15"></a>
### C24-E-C23-15 — Provide actual code-family consumers showing the two premises have different consequences. For the eventual premise, use the noiseless Boolean channel with positive capacity `log 2`, budgets `M n = 4^n`, and constant encoding/decoding: prove actual error `1-1/(4^n)` and rate `2*log 2` for `n>0`; specialize the public pointwise bound with `R=2*log 2` to `Pe_n ≥ 1/2-1/(2*n)`, including the finite nonvacuous instance `n=2`, `M=16`, `Pe≥1/4`, and separately apply the public eventual obstruction. For the frequent premise, retain a zero-capacity constant-output channel with singleton budgets on even lengths and `2^(n*n)` on odd lengths. Prove unbounded rate spikes, infinitely many error-zero lengths and no eventual positive error floor.

**Approved requirements:** `C23.04-SEPARATION`.

Actual families separate the eventual and frequent premises: noiseless Bool with M_n=4^n constant code gives nonvacuous finite/eventual error bounds; zero-capacity constant output with intermittent M_n=1 or 2^(n*n) has unbounded spikes and infinitely many zero errors.

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u, B : Type v; [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B], W : A -> PMF B, actual c : BlockChannelCode A B n M and hM : 1<=M. Output nonemptiness is not an additional public premise. C=channelCapacity W, Pe=c.errorProbability W hM, r=codeRate n M, all information/rates in Real nats. Family data: M : Nat -> Nat, hM : forall n, 1<=M n, c : forall n, BlockChannelCode A B n (M n); one fixed W. e_n=(c n).errorProbability W (hM n), r_n=codeRate n (M n). Limits, eventually and frequently are along Filter.atTop on ALL natural lengths. Concrete finite Bool alphabets; all budgets positive. fourCode has constant false encoder and decoder index0; intermittentCode uses the same total-code recipe on a constant-output channel. Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

**Edges and distinctions:** The positive-capacity finite example is not a vacuous zero-capacity inequality. The frequent family has real code/error objects and no eventual positive error floor. No optimal decoding or achievability classification inferred from these bad codes.

### C24-E-C23-16 — Consume the public coding bound with all four delivered standard capacities. Include one-letter noiseless identity, input-independent output, BSC identity decoding with actual error equal to crossover, and BEC total erasure fallback with actual error equal to half the erasure probability; retain endpoints and at least one interior noisy value.

**Approved requirements:** `C23.05-STANDARD`.

The public finite coding bound is consumed with all four standard capacities and actual one-letter errors: noiseless identity0; input-independent uniform output1/2; BSC identity-decoder epsilon; BEC total fallback epsilon/2.

Noise epsilon : NNReal with epsilon<=1; thus closed parameter interval [0,1]. BEC output is Option Bool and decoder maps none/some false to0, some true to1. Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

**Producer/source connection:** alternating_complete_interface fixes the same code at each n, its actual uniform-message pushforward and triple law. It proves both pair projections and RV/event error identities, error zero, both Markov chains, decoder DPI, uniform-message Fano, message/input DPI, actual-q capacity, undivided finite inequality, guarded normalized/pointwise forms, every tolerance cap, limsup bound and failure of every ordinary real rate limit. Earlier unequal-fiber, correlated collision, standard-noise and intermittent-family consumers retain their own exact law/error facts; no example-private helper is imported into production.

<a id="c24-x-05"></a>
### C24-X-05 — Compactness/continuity and arbitrary-input MI → attained capacity

**Approved requirements:** Cross-chunk integration requirement.

C15 compactness/continuity maximizes the actual channel MI; C18 arbitrary-q marginal bounds and actual products of coordinate maximizers supply both sides of the same C22 capacity equality.

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u and B : Type v, independent universes; [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B], supplied W : A -> PMF B. channelCapacity explicitly binds Nonempty A and equals the Real sSup of the range of p |-> mutualInfo(channelJoint p W) over ALL PMF A. No Nonempty B, DecidableEq, support/positivity, optimizer, compactness or continuity premise is added. Nats. J : Type u, A : J -> Type v, B : J -> Type w with independent index/input/output universes. [Fintype J], [forall j,Fintype(A j)], [forall j,Nonempty(A j)], [forall j,Fintype(B j)] and W : forall j,A j -> PMF(B j). J may be empty. Upper law q : PMF(forall j,A j) is arbitrary/correlated. Lower laws p j are actual selected/supplied maximizing PMFs; no within-pair or output-independence assumption is introduced. A : Type v, B : Type w, independently universed; [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B], W and every n : Nat including0. Objective q : PMF(Fin n -> A) arbitrary. Supplied iid achiever additionally takes p : PMF A and its equality to the SAME one-letter capacity. The base Nonempty A binder remains even at n0; vacuous Empty base is tested separately on inhabited zero-word domains without forming its base capacity. Canonical Real information/capacity in nats; bits only through the explicit existing Units conversion.

**Edges and distinctions:** Actual objective/domain and laws are unchanged across producers. Independent lower witness never narrows the upper supremum domain. No optimizer uniqueness or operational-capacity equality. Earlier X01-X04 applicability remains parent scope.

<a id="c24-x-06"></a>
### C24-X-06 — Actual channel-code law/error/rate → Fano weak converse

**Approved requirements:** Cross-chunk integration requirement.

The actual code law/error and shared nats rate compose with raw memoryless sampling, Markov/DPI, uniform-source Fano and the arbitrary-input repeated capacity bound on the SAME code/family. Derived boundedness precedes real limsup and eventual/frequent obstructions remain distinct.

**Assumptions, universes and units:** A : Type u, B : Type v; [Fintype A] [Nonempty A] [Fintype B], W : A -> PMF B, actual c : BlockChannelCode A B n M and hM : 1<=M. Output nonemptiness is not an additional public premise. C=channelCapacity W, Pe=c.errorProbability W hM, r=codeRate n M, all information/rates in Real nats. Family data: M : Nat -> Nat, hM : forall n, 1<=M n, c : forall n, BlockChannelCode A B n (M n); one fixed W. e_n=(c n).errorProbability W (hM n), r_n=codeRate n (M n). Limits, eventually and frequently are along Filter.atTop on ALL natural lengths. Raw construction uses only finite outputs; stronger capacity hypotheses enter exactly at C(W). Finite/pointwise/vanishing-error/eventual-gap/frequent-gap hypotheses remain distinct. Real natural-log information/rates; explicit positive Nat budgets; original PMF event masses with existing guarded toReal semantics

**Edges and distinctions:** No uniform-on-image or independent-codeword shortcut. No abstract unrelated witness for the operational law or average error. Source strong converse is not channel strong converse. General channel achievability, operational-capacity equality and error->1 remain excluded.
